    d6 is (2, 3).
    Diagonal: (0,5) -> (1,4) -> (2,3).

    (2,3) -> (3,4) -> (4,5) ...
    (2,3) -> (3,2) -> (4,1) ...
    No.

    So c8f5 is a strong move because it checks the king.
    Let's see if there's any better move.
    Black is developed poorly, but White's king is in the center of the board.
    Attacking a centralized king is usually a good strategy.

    Let's verify the legal move `c8f5` one more time.
    c8 (0,2) -> f5 (3,5).
    dx = 3-0 = 3, dy = 5-2 = 3.
    It is a diagonal movement.
    And from f5 (3,5) to e4 (4,4):

    It is a diagonal movement.
    So c8f5+ is a check.

- d3 is not attacked by Pawn f3 (Pawns attack diagonally forward. White pawns move from rank 2 to 8. So a pawn at f3 attacks e4 and g4. Not d3).
- d3 is not attacked by Pawn a2.
So e3d3 is legal.

If Black King moves to e2:
- e2 is not attacked by Rook f4.
- e2 is not attacked by King g4.
- e2 is not attacked by Pawn f3 (attacks e4, g4).
- e2 is not attacked by Pawn a2 (attacks b3).
So e3e2 is legal.

If Black King moves to d2:
- d2 is not attacked by Rook f4.
- d2 is not attacked by King g4.
- d2 is not attacked by Pawn f3.
- d2 is not attacked by Pawn a2 (attacks b3).
So e3d2 is legal.

If Black King moves to f2:
- f2 is attacked by Rook f4? The pawn at f3 blocks the attack from f4 to f2. So f2 is safe from the Rook.
- f2 is not attacked by King g4.
- f2 is not attacked by Pawn f3.
- f2 is not attacked by Pawn a2.
So e3f2 is legal.

The best strategy for Black is to stay active and avoid getting trapped on the edge.
